### 18.3 'Topics no state has a row for yet'
The reference carries no such list; every topic has rows in at least one state and is answered in §18.1 or §18.2.

## 19. Step D screens (rules 40-53), one line each
- **35 constitution:** loaded before the first battery; art. I, §§ 7, 21, 24 and art. XIX screened; only § 21 (jury) reaches a row as a decision (`edu-jury-waiver-wa`).
- **37 tenancy type:** notices and rights differ for month-to-month, fixed-term under (1)(b), fixed-term under (1)(c) and other fixed terms (RCW 59.18.650(1)); tenant notice 20 days (RCW 59.18.200(1), 59.18.650(1)(f)); rent limit applies to every tenancy type; mobile home lots flagged where they differ.
- **39 eviction duties:** post-writ property and storage requests (`edu-post-eviction-property-wa`); no self-help (`edu-self-help-eviction-wa`); limited dissemination of records (`edu-eviction-record-sealing-wa`); right to counsel, reinstatement and hardship stays (`edu-eviction-process-wa`, `edu-eviction-hardship-stay-wa`); state and local court rules read (§1.1, §7).
- **40 formatting and placement:** batteries 117, 180-187; no type-size or bold rule for leases; layout table §4.
- **41 just cause:** RCW 59.18.650, recorded in `edu-for-cause-eviction-wa`; shared end-of-term wording replaced (§2.2).
- **42 required text in a shared clause:** deposit withholding terms (`security-deposit-use-wa`), nonrefundable fees (`nonrefundable-fees-wa`), the fee-in-lieu form, the window-unit notice; each is its own clause.
- **43 cure promises:** `default-by-tenant-wa` promises no cure beyond the statutory 14-day and 10-day notices and keeps the 3-day no-cure ground; `early-termination` (10-day cure for any breach) not tagged.
- **44 terms turned into duties:** landlord-supplied appliances and facilities must be maintained (RCW 59.18.060(8); `appliances-included`); agreed services; agreed utilities (`edu-heat-alert-utility-wa`); the repair remedies reach defects in what the landlord supplies.
- **45 electronic notices:** the electronic records chapter (RCW 1.80.040, 1.80.060, 1.80.070) read for the notice and signature rows; termination and eviction notices follow RCW 59.12.040 and 59.18.055 (`edu-notice-delivery-wa`); electronic-only rent payment barred.
- **46 lease as the notice:** the fire safety notice, landlord identity, flood disclosure, deposit terms, depository notice and window-unit notice can be lease paragraphs; the checklist, screening notice, increase notice, smart access policy and death designation cannot (§4).
- **47 knowing-use penalties:** RCW 59.18.230(3) (actual damages, up to twice the monthly rent, fees; `edu-knowing-use-penalty-wa`); no WA clause contains an RCW 59.18.230(2) term (independent check); Collection Agency Act definition read, its prohibited practices not (§7).
- **48 separate documents:** RCW 59.18.590(1)(b) (death designation), RCW 59.18.360 (exemption agreement), RCW 59.18.670(2)(a) (separate acknowledgment inside the lease); no separate-writing rule for tenant chores.
- **49 collection costs:** RCW 59.18.230(2)(e) and 59.18.410(1); RCW 4.84.330 makes a one-way fee clause reciprocal (`edu-attorney-fees-wa`); collection and notice-service fees not offered.
- **50 'the lease controls':** each choice made on purpose: deposit interest (RCW 59.18.270: statutory default kept), the fixed-term options (RCW 59.18.650(1)(b)-(c): offered as options), late fees only as the lease provides (`late-fee-wa`), rules brought to the tenant's attention at move-in (RCW 59.18.140(1)), the due-date change only where the lease allows late fees (`edu-rent-due-date-change-wa`).
- **51 plain language and consumer contracts:** no plain-language statute for leases (battery 117); CPA in `edu-consumer-protection-act-wa`.
- **52 exculpation:** RCW 59.18.230(2)(f); the ks-oh and ks-oh-ca variants tagged; `pet-policy-wa` without the indemnity.
- **53 figures vs shared clauses:** `late-fee` (grace period), `returned-payments` and `holdover` (ceiling-only), `due-at-signing` (installments), `landlords-access` (24 hours vs two days) replaced; `security-deposit-use` (statutory limits) replaced.
- **54t tenant-caused damage:** answered exit by exit in `tenant-caused-damage-wa` (§6.1).
- **79 summaries re-read:** every row written section-open; qualifiers attached to their own sentences; 15 independent-check rounds and a check of this log (§13). No WA clause lacks a basis.

## Proposed SOP changes
1. Rule 19: run the 'all known positives passed' check over the whole battery log after a run finishes, never on a partial log; Washington's early check read the first records only and missed seven failed batteries, found on the full log and rerun.
2. Rule 80: the tool checkers use to print a section must print the whole section; Washington's cut sections at 6,000 characters, which round 7 found, and 38 rows had to be re-checked against the tail text.
3. Rule 59: the helper that registers a quote should require the quote's full citation next to it in the row; Washington added a script that fails a quote without its full citation within the preceding 160 characters.
4. Rule 19: a battery with a tenancy-word context filter can miss rules written in another regime's vocabulary (Washington's pool rules in chapter 246-260 WAC, the CPA weatherization exception); before citing it for an absence, rerun without the filter or with the regime's own term of art.
5. Rule 30: before writing an absence, read the neighbouring sections of the act; Washington first recorded no landlord self-cure rule while RCW 59.18.180 provides one.
6. Rule 27: run the topic canvass before the independent check, not while writing the log; Washington's water-heater rule (RCW 19.27A.060) sat in battery 40's hits until the canvass, so its row was checked only in the last round.
7. Rule 14: generate a battery record's corpus label from the loaded index; Washington's records say 52,036 RCW sections where the index holds 52,061.

## Proposed topic questions
1. `water-heater-temperature`: Does a statute outside the landlord-tenant act (an energy or building chapter) require the owner to set an individual water heater's temperature at each new occupancy? (Washington, RCW 19.27A.060(3).)
2. `for-cause-eviction`: Does the just-cause statute let a fixed term end without cause only if the lease uses specified options, so that without them the tenancy becomes month-to-month? (Washington, RCW 59.18.650(1)(b)-(d).)
3. `rent-control`: Does a statewide rent-increase limit count recurring fees as rent, and does it expire on a set date? (Washington, RCW 59.18.030, 59.18.700(8).)
4. `security-deposit-holding`: Must the landlord give written notice of the depository, and who keeps trust-account interest unless the lease says otherwise? (Washington, RCW 59.18.270.)
5. `guest-policy`: On mobile home lots, does a live-in care provider statute limit a guest-stay or consent clause, and do lot rules need their own notice and grace period? (Washington, RCW 59.20.145, 59.20.045(6).)
6. `dv-lease-termination`: Does a victim of the landlord have a separate route (leave first, or change locks with the lease ending automatically unless the tenant opts to stay)? (Washington, RCW 59.18.575(3)-(4).)
7. `pool-safety`: Do health-department water recreation rules reach pools at apartments and rental housing, with exclusions for single-family homes and owner-occupied duplexes? (Washington, chapter 246-260 WAC; RCW 70.90.250.)

## Sync (Claude Code, 2026-10-04)

- **Merged** with `merge-delta.py --base 97cfed4` (the 3,175-row library the kickoff was staged from), after the circle-back syncs that ran meanwhile: 40 rows tagged, the 2 dormant WA rows rewritten and activated, 154 new; nothing refused. Library 3,336 rows; WA 196 active (68 lease clauses, 128 education); every other state's set unchanged.
- **Fixed at sync:**
  - `lease-end-continuation-wa` and `fixed-term-end-without-cause-wa` share `automatic-renewal` (§10 item 5). A choice group was tried and taken out: the app requires every choice group to have one default (a backend test enforces it), and neither option may be a default. The eligibility brackets limit each, the notes say a lease uses at most one, and an "at most one, or none" control is a builder gap in the backlog.
  - `pet-policy-wa` pointed to "the Security Deposit section of this Lease", but WA's deposit clauses are titled "Use of Security Deposit" and "Return of Security Deposit"; reworded to "this Lease's provisions on the use and return of the Security Deposit".
  - `assistance-animal-accommodation-wa` was titled "Assistance Animals", which the section-pointer guard matched inside other states' pointers to their "Service and Assistance Animals Section"; retitled "Service and Assistance Animals", the title most states use.
- **Rule 27:** all seven topics have WA rows; no rows added at sync.
- **Citations file:** `lease-clause-citations-WA.csv` built from each row's WA note segment (196 rows: 165 cited, 25 confirmed absent with their batteries, 6 generic).
- **Legal watch:** WA config in `stateConfig.js` (RCW sections only, query paired with "RCW"; 179 sections), federal lead checks, and four manual items (the 2027 and 2028 dated versions, the 2040 rent-limit expiry and yearly maximum, the WAC and court rules). `legal-watch-wa.yml` committed held until after 2027-07-06; first run 2027-08-06, day 6 at 14:00 UTC.
- **Topic questions:** the seven proposed above plus one for each new topic key (`portable-cooling-device`, `lease-type-parity`, `smart-access`).
- **Guards:** all pass. **Statute spot-check, 5 of 5, against the official RCW 59.18 text fetched from app.leg.wa.gov on 2026-10-03:** § 59.18.650(1)(b) (initial term of six to 12 months, notice before the end) and (1)(c) (12 months or more, or successive terms of six months or more); § 59.18.170(2) (no late fee for rent paid within five days of its due date); § 59.18.150(6) (at least two days' written notice of entry, with the date and time window); § 59.18.280(1)(a) (full and specific statement within 30 days).
- **SOP 1.54:** all seven proposals adopted (rules 14, 19, 27, 30, 59, 80); WA column added. The §10 flags are in the backlog (builder inputs, two new variables for M.14, separate documents).
- **Rule 62:** no shared text changed. WA is not tagged on `default-by-tenant` or `surrender-end-of-term`, so no pending vetting reaches it.

## Cross-state decisions, 2026-10-04 (Taylor; central check by Claude Code)

- **New federal row:** `edu-cares-act-notice` is now tagged for WA. Under 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c), a landlord of a property with a federally backed mortgage, or in a covered federal housing program, may not require the tenant to vacate until 30 days after a notice to vacate; whether that still applies after the 2020 moratorium is unsettled. Any WA row that already mentions the CARES Act stays; the federal row is the library's standard explanation.
- **Shared-text edits merged centrally** (Taylor approved merging now; each only narrows the tenant's obligations or defers to applicable law, so it can't breach WA's law; to be confirmed at WA's end-of-run consistency pass):
- `smoking-policy`: Tenant pays for smoking damage caused by "Tenant, an occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant", no longer by anyone's smoking.
- `no-alterations`: the last sentence adds that the clause "does not change who owns an installation that applicable law makes Tenant's property".
- `addendum-precedence`: a disclosure, notice or addendum "that applicable law says controls over this Lease" now controls, as one the law requires already did.

## Federal row: HUD assistance-animal enforcement change (Claude Code, 2026-11-12)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-12: the assistance-animal clause stays as written (an animal a tenant needs for a disability, trained or not, is an accommodation, not a pet), and a new federal education row, `edu-assistance-animals-federal-enforcement`, is tagged here and in every jurisdiction but CO. It explains HUD's September 17, 2025 withdrawal of its assistance-animal guidance (91 Fed. Reg. 17291) and FHEO's May 22, 2026 memorandum limiting Fair Housing Act charges to trained animals, and what neither changes: the Act, 24 C.F.R. § 100.204, private suits, Section 504 and ADA complaints, and state law. No clause text changed.

## Federal rows: foreclosure tenants, screening-report notice, voucher leases (Claude Code, 2026-11-12)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-12 (Ohio gaps product item P1): three federal education rows are tagged here and in every jurisdiction, each read from the official federal text: `edu-ptfa-foreclosure-tenants` (Protecting Tenants at Foreclosure Act, 12 U.S.C. § 5220 note), `edu-fcra-adverse-action-notice` (Fair Credit Reporting Act adverse-action notice, 15 U.S.C. § 1681m) and `edu-voucher-tenancy-addendum` (Housing Choice Voucher lease and termination rules, 24 C.F.R. §§ 982.308, 982.310). This state's existing rows on these laws stay; no clause text changed.

## WA topic gaps: end-of-run consistency pass (2026-11-18/19)

**Date:** 2026-11-19. **SOP 2.15.** Opus, high effort; research mode not used (none of rule 9's triggers arose: the whole RCW, the held WAC titles, the Constitution and the 2025-2026 session laws were loaded and searched directly). **Status:** 178 of 178 topics answered. Delta: 176 new WA rows (3 clauses, 173 education), 9 existing rows tagged WA. Scope fence: no existing row's text changed; tags change only `states` and add a `WA:` segment.

The README was read first. Completeness rests on its proof (all 2,785 indexed RCW chapters fetched; 2,782 of 2,792 chapter files match their own tables of contents section for section, the other ten being empty cross-reference pages; all 105 WAC chapters match). The search index built here holds 57,128 units: 52,685 RCW section entries (every dated version a separate unit, as the README counts them), 2,987 WAC sections (titles 51, 162, 212, 365, 480 whole; chapters 246-260, 246-290, 246-291, 296-150M), 275 Constitution sections plus the whole text, the 691 session laws of 2025 and 2026, 18 federal sections, 443 state court rules and 28 local superior court rule sets. Five section pages were read one at a time in Taylor's built-in browser on 2026-11-19 and compared with the corpus by SHA-256 of the whitespace-free body: RCW 59.18.230, RCW 59.18.280, RCW 19.27A.060 and RCW 35.21.830 match exactly; RCW 59.18.575 matches once the statutory form's dot-leader fill lines are also removed (the PDF and the web page print different numbers of dots in the blanks).

### §3 Batteries for every absence

Battery tool: `bat.py` over the hash-verified corpus (rule 19): body text only, both the plain and end-of-line-joined copies, IGNORECASE and DOTALL, control `zqxjvwk` run in the same call (0 in every battery), a known positive for every term, heading-only matches listed, inflection probe. Ids carry the prefix WAG (the first pass's are cited as "WA log battery N"). Each row cites the generated citation text; the full term list, patterns, scopes, hit lists and positives of every battery are in the battery log (`wag-batteries-<slice>.jsonl`, delivered with this log on request). Absence rows and the batteries they cite:

**Replay before delivery (rule 19).** Every term of every battery above was re-run against the final index (880 terms). 10 returned a different count from the one logged, all because of the five federal sections added after the canvass (§1, §4.3), which carry the searched words:
- WAG4004 T1: logged 24, now 25; added FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.551
- WAG4004 T2: logged 402, now 403; added FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.551
- WAG4005 T1: logged 24, now 25; added FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.551
- WAG6008 T4: logged 16, now 17; added FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.404
- WAG9006 T1: logged 19, now 20; added FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.551
- WAG11000 T1: logged 57, now 58; added FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.404
- WAG12017 T1: logged 12, now 13; added FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.404
- WAG12017 T3: logged 11, now 12; added FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.404
- WAG12024 T2: logged 3, now 4; added FEDERAL: 16 C.F.R. § 682.3
- WAG12025 T3: logged 26, now 27; added FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.551
Each added section was read against its battery's purpose, and none answers it: FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.551 states a voucher family's obligations to the agency (for WAG4004 and WAG4005 it is not a notice of just-cause coverage; for WAG9006 it gives no eviction defense, the owner's bar on terminating for the agency's unpaid share being FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.310(b), already in `edu-social-security-defense-wa`; for WAG12025 it is no purchase right); FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.404 puts appliance supply on the family where the owner isn't required to provide them (WAG6008), makes relocation after a failed inspection the agency's job (WAG11000), and abates the agency's payment without splitting a court's rent reduction (WAG12017, now stated in `edu-no-subsidy-proration-rule-wa`); FEDERAL: 16 C.F.R. § 682.3 is a disposal rule, not a duty to keep or produce records (WAG12024; stated in `edu-tenant-confidential-information-wa`). No absence verdict changes. Main-pass batteries WAG2, WAG5 and WAG6 ran on the final index.

### §4 Decisions and questions asked of Taylor

#### 4.1 Shared rows confirmed (prompt item 8)
- `smoking-policy` (central edit of 2026-10-04): confirmed, carried as written. The cost sentence now reaches smoking 'by Tenant, an occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant'; RCW 59.18.130(4) bars the tenant from damaging the premises 'or permit any member of his or her family, invitee, licensee, or any person acting under his or her control to do so' (RCW 59.18.130(4)), so charging the cost of damage those persons cause is within the tenant's statutory duty; the edit only narrows the earlier unlimited wording. None of RCW 59.18.230(2)(a)-(j) reaches a remediation charge. The charge is a non-rent amount, so 'the tenant's right to possession of the premises may not be conditioned on a tenant's payment or satisfaction of any monetary amount other than rent' (RCW 59.18.283(2)); "a violation may be treated as a default" works only as a substantial breach of a material term under RCW 59.18.650(2)(b) (effective until January 1, 2028; same in the version effective January 1, 2028), as `default-by-tenant-wa` already says.
- `no-alterations` (central edit of 2026-10-04): confirmed. The added sentence ("does not change who owns an installation that applicable law makes Tenant's property") only defers to law. The clause's last sentence preserves the reasonable modification Washington requires a landlord to permit 'at the expense of the person with a disability' (RCW 49.60.222(2)(a)) and FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 100.203, and the portable cooling right: 'a landlord may not prohibit or restrict a tenant from installing a portable cooling device of the tenant's choosing' (RCW 59.18.740(1)), with RCW 59.18.740(2)'s bar on any fee 'for the use, inspection, or installation of a portable cooling device' (RCW 59.18.740(2)). Washington has no EV-charging, solar or telecom installation right for tenants (`edu-no-ev-charging-rule-wa`, `edu-no-portable-solar-rule-wa`, `edu-no-telecom-access-rule-wa`, `edu-no-display-rule-wa`), so the ownership sentence has nothing to bite on in Washington today.
- `addendum-precedence` (central edit of 2026-10-04): confirmed. Under the new wording, the documents Washington requires control over a conflicting lease term: the fire safety notice (RCW 59.18.060(12), `fire-safety-notice-wa`), the mold information (RCW 59.18.060(14), `mold-disclosure-wa`), the flood disclosure for leases entered into after December 31, 2026 (RCW 59.18.060(13), `flood-disclosure-wa`), the landlord's name and address for notices (RCW 59.18.060(16), `landlord-disclosure-wa`), the fee-in-lieu disclosure form (RCW 59.18.670(1)(f), `fee-in-lieu-of-deposit-wa`) and the federal lead disclosure (FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 4852d; FEDERAL: 40 C.F.R. § 745.113). Each is a "disclosure, notice, or addendum that applicable law requires".
- `hoa-compliance`: confirmed, no override. An association may 'levy reasonable fines against the tenant and unit owner for the violation' after notice and an opportunity to be heard (RCW 64.90.405(5)(b)), so a fine incurred for the tenant's violation is a loss the tenant caused. Making the tenant responsible for it is not a prohibited term (RCW 59.18.230(2)(a)-(j)); as a non-rent amount it can't condition possession (RCW 59.18.283(2)) and comes out of a deposit only under the lease's stated terms and with RCW 59.18.280(1)'s statement.
- `keys`: confirmed, no override. A rekey charge for unreturned keys is damages the tenant caused; it is not a prohibited term, can't condition possession (RCW 59.18.283(2)), and comes out of a deposit only with the documentation RCW 59.18.280(1)(b) requires (copies of estimates or invoices, or the landlord's time and hourly rate). No stated amount is needed: the clause charges actual cost, which is the measure RCW 59.18.280(1)(b) documents.

#### 4.2 Early termination, casualty fault persons and CARES (prompt item 9)
- (a) `early-termination-wa`: confirmed. The fee is charged only when 'Tenant may end this Lease before the end of the Term by giving Landlord at least 30 days' written notice and paying' the fee, the option the clause offers; it adds that "Nothing in this Section limits any termination right Tenant has under applicable law", naming military orders, victims, threatening behavior by Landlord and unremedied defects. So no fee falls on a statutory termination: servicemembers (RCW 59.18.200(1)(b), RCW 59.18.220(2)), victims (RCW 59.18.575), threats (RCW 59.18.352, threats by another tenant with a deadly weapon followed by an arrest and no eviction filing within seven days; RCW 59.18.354, threats by the landlord), and defects (RCW 59.18.090(1), RCW 59.18.120). Federal: under the SCRA 'The lessor may not impose an early termination charge' (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(e)(1)); the clause charges none on that route. The clause's illustrative list names landlord threats but not RCW 59.18.352's threats by another tenant; the general sentence preserves both, so no change.
- (b) Casualty fault persons: the same persons. `tenant-caused-damage-wa` names "Tenant, a member of Tenant's family, an invitee or another person acting under Tenant's control", the persons in 'caused by the conduct of such tenant, his or her family, invitee, or other person acting under his or her control' (RCW 59.18.060); `edu-casualty-termination-wa` says "the tenant, family, guest or anyone under the tenant's control". "Guest" is the plain word for the statute's "invitee"; the persons match. A wording alignment is offered under §6 (optional).
- (c) CARES: no WA eviction row contradicts `edu-cares-act-notice`. The rows state Washington's own periods (14-day pay-or-vacate, 10-day comply, 3-day quit, the cause-based periods) and none says a state notice is the only notice the law requires. Where a property is covered by FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058, the federal row's cautious course (a 30-day notice to vacate before filing) sits on top of each, including the 3-day no-cure route in `edu-expedited-criminal-eviction-wa` and `default-by-tenant-wa`.


#### 4.3 Canvass decisions (rule 54 options, drafting judgments, builder items)

- [A] showing-notice-waiver (rule 54, barred option): the shared optional clause `showing-notice-la` (a shorter lease-set notice for showings) is screened and not tagged for Washington, because 'The tenant shall not unreasonably withhold consent to the landlord to enter the dwelling unit at a specified time where the landlord has given at least one day's notice of intent to enter to exhibit the dwelling unit to prospective or actual purchasers or tenants.' (RCW 59.18.150(6)) sets a floor a lease cannot lower (RCW 59.18.230(1)(a)). `landlords-access-wa` already carries the one-day showing notice; the new `edu-showing-notice-waiver-wa` records the bar. No new variable.
- [A] liquidated-damages (rule 54, lawful option declined): no Washington statute governs liquidated damages in a residential lease (statute-wide search in `edu-liquidated-damages-wa`), so a general liquidated-damages clause is not barred by statute, but the library declines it: late fees within five days are barred (RCW 59.18.230(2)(i)), judgments carry late fees only 'if such fees are due under the lease and do not exceed $75 in total' (RCW 59.18.410(1)), abandonment damages follow RCW 59.18.310(1)(b) net of mitigation, deposit damage charges must be documented (RCW 59.18.280(3)(b)), and the penalty doctrine was not searched. The tenant's early-termination option (`early-termination-wa`) stays the library's only agreed-sum term. Education row `edu-liquidated-damages-wa` says a landlord may add their own clause with advice. No new variable.
- [A] repair-notice (rule 54, lawful option declined): a lease clause telling the tenant how to send repair requests is lawful only if it adds to, and never narrows, the statutory notice ('deliver written notice to the person designated in *RCW 59.18.060(14), or to the person who collects the rent' (RCW 59.18.070)). The library offers none for Washington: the statute already fixes the notice, its recipients and contents, a narrower procedure would be unenforceable (RCW 59.18.230(1)(a)), and `notices` with `landlord-disclosure-wa` already give the address. Notice value alone is not a basis (rule 55). Education row `edu-repair-notice-wa` records it. No new variable.
- [A] maintenance-duty-shift (rule 54, lawful option declined): a clause recording a tenant's agreement to do repairs for 'cash payment or a reasonable reduction in rent' (RCW 59.18.100(6)) is lawful but 'does not alter the landlord's obligations under this chapter' (RCW 59.18.100(6)). The library declines it: the consideration must be negotiated for the specific work, it shifts no duty, and a template version risks being read as a waiver of RCW 59.18.060 duties, which is unenforceable outside an RCW 59.18.360 agreement (not in a standard form lease). Tagged chore clauses (`tenant-maintenance`, `landscaping-irrigation`, `snow-removal`) stay as they are. Education row `edu-maintenance-duty-shift-wa` says a landlord may add their own clause with advice. No new variable.
- [A] smoke-drift-waiver (rule 54, barred option): shared single-state drift-waiver clauses (`smoke-drift-waiver-ut`, `smoke-drift-waiver-wy`) rest on their own states' statutes and are not considered for tagging; a Washington drift waiver releasing the landlord would be a prohibited term ('Agrees to the exculpation or limitation of any liability of the landlord arising under law or to indemnify the landlord for that liability or the costs connected therewith' (RCW 59.18.230(2)(f))). No clause is offered; `smoking-policy` (tagged) is the lawful route. Education row `edu-no-smoke-drift-waiver-wa`.
- [A] binding-arbitration (item 10; Taylor's decision 2026-11-07, applied, not re-asked): Washington allows a lease arbitration term only if the landlord pays the entire cost, the agreement is notarized and no particular arbitrator is agreed at signing (RCW 59.18.230(2)(g)-(h)), and RCW 59.18.230(3) penalizes knowingly using a prohibited term ('statutory damages not to exceed two times the monthly rent charged for the unit' (RCW 59.18.230(3))). Under Taylor's rule (offer only where state law allows it and no statute penalizes including a prohibited term) the shared `arbitration-agreement` is not offered or tagged for Washington; it would itself be prohibited here (named arbitrator at signing; landlord pays only fees above the court filing fee; no notarization). Education row `edu-binding-arbitration-wa` explains the conditions and penalty and says a landlord may add their own clause with advice. The reading of the statutes matches item 10; nothing for §4.
- [B] dv-deposit-timing: no clause offered. RCW 59.18.575(2)(b)(i) operates notwithstanding any lease forfeiture term, so a lease clause would only restate it; a forfeiture-for-early-termination clause cannot be applied against a tenant who terminates under RCW 59.18.575. The co-tenant division gap is stated in the row with a cautious course rather than drafted around.
- [B] dv-protection-order-chapter-moved: the gaps found in RCW 59.18.575(1)(a)(i)'s order list (vulnerable adult and extreme risk orders under RCW 7.105.100(1)(d)-(e); pre-July 1, 2022 orders under chapter 74.34 RCW and the former chapters 7.92 and 7.94 RCW kept valid by RCW 7.105.550(2); foreign orders valid under RCW 26.52.020) are treated as interpretive doubts with a cautious course (accept any valid protective order or the third-party record), not as NEEDS_REVIEW. The printing slip in RCW 59.18.570(4) (a letter l for the digit 1 in its cross-reference to RCW 59.18.030) is noted in the row; no lease clause relies on it.
- [B] carpet-cleaning (rule 54): (a) a fixed carpet-cleaning charge against the deposit, or a professional-cleaning charge applied to the deposit without documented excess wear, is barred by RCW 59.18.280(1)(c)(ii) and unenforceable under RCW 59.18.230(1)(a); recorded as barred in `edu-carpet-cleaning-wa`. (b) A clause requiring the tenant to have the carpets professionally cleaned at move-out, with no deposit charge, is not authorized by any statute and its cost could not be recovered for ordinary-use soiling (RCW 59.18.280(3)(b)); declined as doubtful, education row says the landlord may add their own clause with advice. (c) The lawful alternative, a written nonrefundable cleaning fee (RCW 59.18.285), is already offered by `nonrefundable-fees-wa`. The shared clause `carpet-cleaning-ar` is not tagged for WA.
- [B] deposit-cost-schedule (rule 54): a lease schedule of fixed cleaning, repair or replacement charges against the deposit is treated as barred to the extent it would let the landlord withhold more than the documented actual cost (RCW 59.18.280(1)(b)-(c), RCW 59.18.230(1)(a)); an informational price list or hourly rate is lawful but declined, because each deduction must still rest on RCW 59.18.280(1)(b) documents and a list is easily read as a pre-set charge. The education row says a landlord may add their own clause after review. The lawful set charge is a nonrefundable cleaning fee (`nonrefundable-fees-wa`).
- [B] deposit-surrender-notice (rule 54): a lease condition making advance notice of surrender (or a forwarding address) a condition of the deposit refund is barred in Washington as a waiver of RCW 59.18.280 (RCW 59.18.230(1)(a), RCW 59.18.230(2)(a)); recorded as barred in `edu-no-deposit-surrender-notice-wa`. The request for a forwarding address, without any condition, is already in `security-deposit-return-wa`.
- [B] expedited-deposit-disposition (rule 54): a Virginia-style fee for expedited deposit processing is declined in Washington: no statute authorizes it, the statement and refund are the landlord's own duty under RCW 59.18.280, and its lawfulness is unsettled; the education row says it is not offered and a landlord may add their own clause only with advice and the tenant's separate written agreement. Early disposition without a fee needs no clause.
- [C] required-fees (rule 26 tag screen): the shared clause `fees-and-charges-vi` is not tagged for Washington. It lists fees without requiring that a nonrefundable fee be marked nonrefundable, and a Washington fee not so marked 'must be treated as a refundable deposit' (RCW 59.18.285) under the deposit sections (RCW 59.18.260, RCW 59.18.270 and RCW 59.18.280), so a landlord filling its bracket could lose the fee; it does not flag that recurring charges are rent under RCW 59.18.030 (increase notice and limit); and its upfront list overlaps `due-at-signing-wa` and `nonrefundable-fees-wa`, which already carry Washington's rules. The single-state rows (`required-fees-co`, `required-fees-mn`, `required-fees-nv`, `fees-and-charges-nd` and others) rest on their own statutes and are not tagged. Education row `edu-required-fees-wa` written instead. No new variable.
- [C] governmental-fines (rule 54, option declined): a clause making the tenant reimburse government fines or abatement costs caused by the tenant is not offered for Washington. The shared clauses `tenant-caused-fines-as` and `tenant-caused-fines-oh` are not tagged: their fault persons ("a member of Tenant's household, or a person on the Premises with Tenant's consent") are not Washington's 'his or her family, invitee, or other person acting under his or her control' (RCW 59.18.060), and a reimbursement promise for a fine imposed on the landlord under law risks the indemnity bar in RCW 59.18.230(2)(f), which only case law could settle. The single-state rows (`governmental-fines-co`, `governmental-fines-az`, `governmental-fines-id`, `governmental-fines-ny`, `tenant-caused-fines-fl` and others) rest on their own law. Education row `edu-governmental-fines-wa` says the landlord may recover actual damages from a tenant's breach and may add a lawyer-drafted clause. No new variable.
- [C] government-fee-reimbursement (rule 54, option declined): no government-fee pass-through clause is offered for Washington. The shared `government-fee-reimbursement-ky` is not tagged: it lets the landlord bill a fee "within [number] days after Landlord's written notice", but a recurring reimbursement identified in the lease is rent under RCW 59.18.030, so a new or higher amount needs ninety days of prior written notice (RCW 59.18.140(3)(a)) on the RCW 59.18.720 form and is limited by RCW 59.18.700(1)(a); the clause would give those rights away. The single-state rows (`government-fee-reimbursement-in`, `-mn`, `-mp`, `-nd`, `-ny` and others) rest on their own law. The lawful route is a fixed recurring charge stated in the lease at the start (rent) or building the fee into rent. Education row `edu-government-fee-reimbursement-wa`. No new variable.
- [C] alarm-tampering-fee (rule 26 tag screen and rule 54): the shared `detector-care-la` is not tagged for Washington. Screened both ways: its duties (do not remove, disable or cover a detector; report a missing or failing one) impose nothing Washington law forbids and give away no tenant right, but its charge sentence reaches "Tenant, or a person on the Premises with Tenant's consent", while Washington's fault persons are 'such tenant, his or her family, invitee, or other person acting under his or her control' (RCW 59.18.060), the persons `tenant-caused-damage-wa` already uses for repair costs; and Washington leaves the actual costs of smoke detector repair or replacement to the advance agreement recorded in `fire-safety-notice-wa` (WAC 212-10-050), which the shared clause could contradict where the landlord chose to pay. A fixed tampering fee is declined (no statute; penalty risk; RCW 59.18.280 documentation). Single-state rows `alarm-tampering-fee-or`, `alarm-tampering-fee-mn`, `alarm-tampering-fee-oh`, `alarm-tampering-cost-in`, `detector-care-ut` and `detector-care-mi` rest on their own law. Education row `edu-no-alarm-tampering-fee-wa`. No new variable.
- [C] collection-fee (rule 54, option declined): no collection-fee clause is offered for Washington. No statute authorizes a landlord's collection fee on a residential tenant (battery in `edu-collection-fee-wa`); RCW 59.18.230(2)(e) bars lease attorney-fee terms beyond the chapter's; a licensed collection agency may add contractual collection costs only to a commercial claim, capped at thirty-five percent (RCW 19.16.250(21)), and a residential tenancy debt is not a commercial claim on Claude's reading of RCW 19.16.100(6); RCW 59.18.280(3)(b) and RCW 59.18.670(3)-(6) restrict collection of undocumented damages and fee-in-lieu losses. Single-state clauses (`collection-fee-ut`, `collection-fee-id`, `collection-costs-ma`, `collection-fee-oh`, `collection-fee-az` and others) rest on their own law and are not tagged. Education row `edu-collection-fee-wa` says a landlord may add their own clause with advice. RCW 19.16.250, read for the first time in this pass (WA log §7 item 5), is now read whole, including the 2025 amendments. No new variable.
- [C] notice-service-fee (rule 54, option declined): no clause charging the tenant for serving a notice or filing an eviction is offered for Washington: no statute authorizes it (battery in `edu-no-notice-service-fee-wa`); the 14-day notice is limited to rent and recurring charges (RCW 59.18.057(1), RCW 59.18.283(2)); judgment and reinstatement amounts are capped (RCW 59.18.410(1)-(2)) and an unlawful detainer agreement exceeding them is void (RCW 59.18.230(1)(b)); costs are court-awarded (RCW 4.84.010); lease attorney-fee terms are barred beyond the chapter's (RCW 59.18.230(2)(e)). Single-state clauses (`notice-service-fee-ut`, `notice-service-fee-id`, `notice-service-fee-ct`, `notice-service-fee-oh`, `eviction-notice-cost-ks`, `notice-cost-fl`) rest on their own law. The education row says a landlord may add their own clause with advice. No new variable.
- [C] tax-escalation (rule 54, barred option): a tax or insurance escalation clause is not offered for Washington and the shared `tax-insurance-escalation-la` is not tagged: it makes the tenant pay a share of each year's increase "during the Term" on the landlord's notice, but a recurring charge identified in the lease is rent (RCW 59.18.030, Claude's reading), and 'any increase in the amount of rent may not become effective prior to the completion of the term of the rental agreement' (RCW 59.18.140(3)(a)), with the 90-day form notice (RCW 59.18.720) and the limit (RCW 59.18.700(1)(a)). Single-state rows (`tax-escalation-ma`, `tax-escalation-ri`, `tax-escalation-nh`, `tax-escalation-de`, `tax-escalation-oh`, `tax-escalation-id`, `tax-escalation-ny`, `tax-insurance-escalation-nd`, `tax-insurance-escalation-il`, `tax-insurance-escalation-mp`, `lessee-property-tax-liability-il`) rest on their own law. Education row `edu-tax-escalation-wa`. No new variable.
- [C] utility-allowance-cap (rule 54, option declined): no utility allowance-and-overage clause is offered for Washington; `utility-allowance-cap-co` stays untagged for the reason the first pass gave (it says the overage "will not be characterized as Rent", while recurring utility charges identified in the lease are rent under RCW 59.18.030), and the other allowance clauses (`utility-allowance-cap-pr`, `-vi`, `-ks`, `-mn`, `-oh`, `-va`, `-ut`, `-il`, `-ok`, `-mi`, `-mt`, `utility-allowance-ia`, `utility-allowance-overage-ne`, `utility-cost-allocation-wy`) rest on their own law. Education row `edu-utility-allowance-cap-wa` states the cautious course. No new variable.
- [C] rent-installments (rule 26): the shared optional clause `rent-installments-or` is tagged for Washington as written. Screened both ways: no Washington statute regulates scheduled rent installments; the clause's only charge reference defers to the lease's late-fee clause, which for Washington is `late-fee-wa` with its five-day sentence (RCW 59.18.170(2)); it takes no tenant right. The week-to-week question under RCW 59.18.200(1)(a) is recorded in the tag segment as an interpretive doubt with no practical change to the tenant's 20-day notice. The single-state variants (`rent-installments-mn`, `-nd`, `-oh`, `-nv`, `-nc`, `-mi`, `prorated-rent-mn`) rest on their own law. No new row, no new variable.
- [C] rent-escalation (rule 54, barred option): no rent escalation, scheduled step-up or mid-term increase clause is offered for Washington, and the shared `rent-escalation-la`, `rent-escalation-ga`, `rent-increase-midterm-ky` and the single-state variants are not tagged: 'any increase in the amount of rent may not become effective prior to the completion of the term of the rental agreement' (RCW 59.18.140(3)(a)), with the first-12-month bar and limit in RCW 59.18.700(1)(a). Education row `edu-rent-escalation-wa` points to the existing increase rows. No new variable.
- [C] lockout-for-rent-delinquency (rule 54, barred option): a lock-change or lockout clause for delinquent rent is barred in Washington by RCW 59.18.290(1) and RCW 59.18.230(1)(a), with utility shutoffs barred by RCW 59.18.300 and distress abolished by RCW 59.18.230(4); the Texas clause `lockout-rent-delinquency-tx` rests on Texas law and is not tagged. Education row `edu-lockout-for-rent-delinquency-wa` quotes the bar and points to `edu-self-help-eviction-wa`. No new variable.
- [C] Assembly note (slice C): `edu-statutory-caps-wa` notes carry a parenthetical, "not quoted here because the quote checker cannot resolve Title 62A section numbers", left in because the saved line may be edited only to fix a check error; the main pass may strip that parenthetical at assembly. Cause: check.py's citation pattern does not match Uniform Commercial Code section numbers such as RCW 62A.3-515 or RCW 62A.3-530, so a single-quoted passage from Title 62A RCW is reported as a quote without a citation; slice C therefore paraphrases Title 62A text (read whole) instead of quoting it.
- [D] good-cause-notice: battery WAG4004 (lease, addendum or other notice telling a tenant whether the tenancy is covered by or exempt from the cause requirement; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 24 hits (real positive passed); T2 402 hits (real positive passed); T3 3 hits (synthetic positive passed); T4 2 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0) is not relied on: its notice-word term T2 returned 402 hits ("good cause" in court and agency deadline rules) that could not be read one by one, so it was rerun with coverage and exemption words in place of notice words, rerun as WAG4005.
- [D] tenant-rights-statement: skipped as instructed; `python3 lib.py wa` lists the active WA row `edu-no-tenant-rights-statement-wa` keyed tenant-rights-statement, so no new row or topic object is written for it in slice D.
- [D] Brief/item 6 wording: "RCW 35.21.830 and its county and code-city counterparts". The corpus has a county counterpart (RCW 36.01.130) but no separate code-city section on rent control: battery T5 of WAG4003 (state preemption of local regulation of landlords, tenants or rental housing; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 26 hits (real positive passed); T2 101 hits (real positive passed); T3 22 hits (real positive passed); T4 16 hits (real positive passed); T5 54 hits (real positive passed); control 0) ("statewide significance") hits only RCW 35.21.830 and RCW 36.01.130 among landlord-tenant sections. Code cities reach the city rule through 'A code city organized or reorganized under this title shall have all of the powers which any city of any class may have and shall be governed in matters of state concern by statutes applicable to such cities in connection with such powers to the extent to which such laws are appropriate and are not in conflict with the provisions specifically applicable to code cities.' (RCW 35A.21.160), read with 'No city or town of any class may enact, maintain, or enforce ordinances or other provisions which regulate the amount of rent' (RCW 35.21.830). No published Washington case on that reading was searched. By contrast, the unrelated-occupants rule does have separate city, code-city and county sections (RCW 35.21.682, RCW 35A.21.314, RCW 36.01.227). No row change needed; `edu-rent-increase-limit-wa` already cites RCW 35.21.830 and RCW 36.01.130 only.
- [D] Rule 54 options, slice D: (1) lease-registration: no memorandum-of-lease clause is offered; recording is optional, needs a separate acknowledged instrument and matters only for terms over two years (RCW 65.08.060, RCW 65.08.070), so the row tells the landlord to record a memorandum with advice. (2) good-cause-notice: no lease statement of cause coverage or exemption is offered; no statute requires one and a wrong statement could mislead a tenant about rights under RCW 59.18.650. (3) accessory-dwelling-unit: the shared optional clause `accessory-dwelling-unit-term-co` (SERVES_LANDLORD) is offered by tagging WA rather than drafting a WA clause, since its text states nothing Washington law contradicts and imposes no charge. (4) No other slice D topic has a lawful optional clause worth offering; each is an education row. No new variables.
- [D] Battery bookkeeping, slice D (each battery's generated citation follows). Rerun for an inflection warning on "void": WAG4001 (duty to record or register a residential lease, and the effect of an unrecorded lease on later buyers or lenders; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 18 hits (real positive passed); T2 14 hits (real positive passed); T3 4 hits (synthetic positive passed); T4 0 hits (synthetic positive passed); T5 18 hits (synthetic positive passed); T6 12 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0); WAG4002 (duty to record or register a residential lease, and the effect of an unrecorded lease on later buyers or lenders; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 18 hits (real positive passed); T2 14 hits (real positive passed); T3 4 hits (synthetic positive passed); T4 0 hits (synthetic positive passed); T5 18 hits (synthetic positive passed); T6 16 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0). Replaced because T2 returned 402 hits that could not be read one by one (not relied on): WAG4004 (lease, addendum or other notice telling a tenant whether the tenancy is covered by or exempt from the cause requirement; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 24 hits (real positive passed); T2 402 hits (real positive passed); T3 3 hits (synthetic positive passed); T4 2 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0); replacement WAG4005 (lease, addendum or other notice telling a tenant whether the tenancy is covered by or exempt from the cause requirement; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 24 hits (real positive passed); T2 83 hits (synthetic positive passed); T3 3 hits (synthetic positive passed); T4 2 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0). The rows rely on WAG4007 (state-published rights and responsibilities guide a landlord must give a tenant before signing or at renewal, with an acknowledgment; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 30 hits (real positive passed); T2 9 hits (synthetic positive passed); T3 14 hits (synthetic positive passed); T4 1 hit (real positive passed); T5 0 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0); WAG4012 (state lead-based paint notices, certificates or packets a landlord must give with a lease or to tenants, beyond the federal disclosure; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules / whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws and saved state and local court rules; T1 13 hits (real positive passed); T2 51 hits (real positive passed); T3 19 hits (real positive passed); T4 10 hits (synthetic positive passed); T5 10 hits (real positive passed); control 0); WAG4016 (duty to disclose a certificate of occupancy, its status or the legal occupancy of a unit to a tenant or in a lease; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 18 hits (real positive passed); T2 65 hits (real positive passed); T3 27 hits (real positive passed); T4 3 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0). - [E] municipal-utility-lien: answered with its own education row, `edu-municipal-utility-lien-wa`, which points to `edu-utility-landlord-account-wa` for the owner-notice mechanics rather than repeating them. No clause: no statute requires lease text, and the shared `utilities-responsibility` clause already allocates payment. The existing row's note that the lien sections RCW 35.21.290 and RCW 35.67.200 were not read is now closed by this row (both read section-open; WA log section 7 item 5). No new variable.
- [E] utility-apportionment (rule 26 screen): the shared optional clause `utility-billing-wv` (WV;AL) is not tagged for Washington. It lets the landlord bill any listed utility by "submetering equipment / energy allocation equipment" or a ratio formula with no carve-out for gas, but 'Under no circumstances will gas be remetered or submetered by a customer for resale to another or others.' (WAC 480-90-108(5)) and electricity may be resold only under the utility's tariff (WAC 480-100-108(5)); and it treats the allocated charges as an ordinary lease obligation without the Washington consequence that recurring utility charges in the lease are rent (RCW 59.18.030) subject to the rent increase rules. A Washington allocation clause would need a gas exclusion, a tariff check for electricity and an increase-notice mechanism; the library declines it for now and answers the topic with `edu-utility-apportionment-wa` (rule 54: a landlord may add their own allocation clause with advice). No new variable.
- [E] meter-conservation-charge: new Washington statute found (RCW 80.28.510, Laws of 2026, ch. 136, effective June 11, 2026; the first pass's "Not applicable: South Carolina rule" predates it). Education row `edu-meter-conservation-charge-wa` plus an optional disclosure clause `meter-conservation-charge-wa` (SERVES_LANDLORD), drafted from the Illinois and Virginia optional clauses' structure but on Washington's statute; bracketed prompts only, no new variable. Builder note: offer the clause only when the landlord says an energy savings charge applies. Legal-watch item: the commission has not yet adopted rules under RCW 80.28.510 in the held WAC Title 480; recheck at the next pass.
- [E] utility-transfer (rule 54): a landlord cutoff or account closure for failure to transfer is barred (RCW 59.18.300); recorded in `edu-utility-transfer-wa`. The shared optional clause `utility-transfer-nj` (no cutoff, reimbursement of the provider's actual charges) is lawful in substance but is not tagged for Washington: its sentence "This reimbursement is not Rent" labels a charge that, if it recurs under the lease, may be rent under RCW 59.18.030 (both versions), the same ground on which the first pass declined `utility-allowance-cap-co`; whether a mid-lease reimbursement for an untransferred account is one of the 'recurring and periodic charges identified in the rental agreement' (RCW 59.18.030(29)) is not settled by the text (no case law searched). The library declines a Washington transfer clause for now; a Washington variant would drop the "not Rent" sentence and say how the reimbursement is collected. `utilities-responsibility` already requires the tenant to arrange and pay for the tenant's utilities. No new variable.
- [E] Slice E scope statement (municipal utilities): the rows' statement that city, town and public utility district utilities are outside the commission's rules rests on WAC 480-100-003(1), WAC 480-90-003(1) and WAC 480-110-255(2), and for cities and towns also on 'Nothing in this title authorizes the commission to make or enforce any order affecting rates, tolls, rentals, contracts or charges or service rendered' (RCW 80.04.500) by a city- or town-owned gas plant, electrical plant, sewer or water system (read section-open after the rows were saved; it confirms them). Public utility districts are not within the RCW 80.04.010(12)(a) definition of electrical company, which names only cities and towns among public owners.
- [E] Currency (rule 16), slice E: the 2025-2026 session laws were screened through each relied-on section's history line. Acts touching these rows: Laws of 2026, ch. 136 (new RCW 80.28.510, effective June 11, 2026); Laws of 2026, ch. 137 (reporting removal; amended RCW 35.21.302, RCW 57.08.081, RCW 54.16.285, RCW 87.03.015, RCW 23.86.405, RCW 24.06.605 and RCW 80.28.010 without changing the tenant, owner-notice or heat-alert text relied on); Laws of 2026, ch. 184 (new RCW 59.18.740, effective June 11, 2026); Laws of 2026, ch. 234 (RCW 59.18.060, flood disclosure); Laws of 2025, ch. 264 (RCW 19.27A.170, RCW 19.27A.200, RCW 19.27A.250); Laws of 2026, ch. 55 (RCW 59.18.030 version effective January 1, 2027, rent definition unchanged). Tool note for the main pass: the filtered currency screen lists Laws of 2026, ch. 137 as amending only RCW 35.21.302 and RCW 80.28.010; its own enacting clause also amends RCW 57.08.081, RCW 54.16.285, RCW 87.03.015, RCW 23.86.405 and RCW 24.06.605, which fall outside the screen's chapter filter.
- [F] Correction before hand-back: the saved row `edu-fire-sprinkler-duty-wa` first said the only owner exemption from sprinkler contractor licensing was the owner-occupier of a single-family residence (RCW 18.160.020(2)(f)). Reading chapter 212-80 WAC for the sprinkler-disclosure topic showed a further rule exemption, 'Full-time employee, or owner of a facility who is qualified to the satisfaction of the authority having jurisdiction to perform fire protection sprinkler work in said facility.' (WAC 212-80-015(2)(g)), and an owner record-keeping duty (WAC 212-80-088(3)). The row's line was corrected in place (bodyText and notes) rather than left wrong; no other field changed.
- [F] common-area-lighting: the shared optional clause `common-area-lighting-ks` (KS;SC;UT;MI;NM) is not tagged WA. Its maintenance sentence, "Landlord shall exercise reasonable care in the maintenance of the shared areas", is the Kansas statutory standard its notes cite, not Washington's, which is to keep shared areas 'reasonably clean, sanitary, and safe from defects increasing the hazards of fire or accident' (RCW 59.18.060(3)); its CONSTRAINED_TERM basis rests on another state's statute; and no Washington statute or rule governs the arrangement (the common-area lighting battery cited in that row, terms T3 to T5). Rule 54: the option (tenant pays for shared-area lights on the tenant's own service) appears lawful in Washington but is declined; `edu-common-area-lighting-wa` says so and tells the landlord a clause can be added with advice.
- [F] Battery housekeeping (slice F): WAG6003 (sprinkler disclosure to tenants and grants, loans, tax credits or exemptions for sprinkler installation or retrofit; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 7 hits (real positive passed); T2 12 hits (real positive passed); T3 15 hits (real positive passed); T4 4 hits (real positive passed); control 0) was rerun for the INFLECTION warning on "notice"; the first rerun, WAG6004 (sprinkler disclosure to tenants and grants, loans, tax credits or exemptions for sprinkler installation or retrofit; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 7 hits (real positive passed); T2 12 hits (real positive passed); T3 15 hits (real positive passed); T4 4 hits (real positive passed); control 0), repeated the old pattern because a text substitution failed, so the inflected rerun is WAG6005 (sprinkler disclosure to tenants and grants, loans, tax credits or exemptions for sprinkler installation or retrofit; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 7 hits (real positive passed); T2 12 hits (real positive passed); T3 15 hits (real positive passed); T4 4 hits (real positive passed); control 0), which `edu-sprinkler-disclosure-wa` cites. WAG6014 (facility-specific landlord duties for building facilities (garage door openers, laundries, recreation and other amenities, elevators); whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 6 hits (real positive passed); T2 1 hit (real positive passed); T3 18 hits (real positive passed); T4 85 hits (real positive passed); control 0) was superseded because its T4 term "conveyance" also caught real-property conveyances (85 hits); the elevator-only rerun is WAG6015 (facility-specific landlord duties for building facilities (garage door openers, laundries, recreation and other amenities, elevators); whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 6 hits (real positive passed); T2 1 hit (real positive passed); T3 18 hits (real positive passed); T4 29 hits (real positive passed); control 0). - [F] Brief correction (slice F): the brief and task message list the IPMC among the model codes that WAC title 51 adopts by reference. It is not adopted statewide: RCW 19.27.031(1) adopts the International Building, Residential, Mechanical and Fire Codes, part of the International Wildland Urban Interface Code, the Uniform Plumbing Code and the accessibility rules, and the council's adoption rules are WAC 51-50-003, WAC 51-51-003 and WAC 51-54A-003 (2021 IBC, IRC and IFC). The IPMC appears only as a code that alterations must also satisfy in the residential energy code, 'International Property Maintenance Code, and NFPA 70' (WAC 51-11R-50100), and in that code's referenced-standards table (WAC 51-11R-51000). Rows in this slice say the IPMC is not a statewide code and that a city or county may adopt its own maintenance code (municipal codes not held).
- [G] asbestos-hazards / local disclosure ordinances: a city or county rule requiring landlords to give tenants information about the property or surrounding area is effective only after posting under RCW 64.06.080(1), and the municipal research contractor's website 'must list by jurisdiction all applicable requirements' (RCW 43.110.030(2)(e)(ii)). No existing WA row cites either section; the asbestos row points landlords to that list. Suggested for the `required-disclosures` summary row (slice owner) as the place to check local tenant-disclosure duties.
- [G] bed-bug-cooperation (rule 54, option offered): optional Washington clause `pest-cooperation-wa` (report, allow entry under `landlords-access-wa`, follow preparation instructions, pay extermination and fumigation costs for an infestation caused by the tenant's conduct or that of family, invitees or persons under the tenant's control). The shared `pest-cooperation-pr` is not tagged: its fault persons differ from RCW 59.18.060's and its "Otherwise, Landlord will pay for treatment" sentence overstates the single-family duty under RCW 59.18.060(4). No new variable. `edu-pest-control-wa` stays the education row for the subject (its own topic `bed-bug-disclosure`).
- [G] hazardous-contamination-disclosure (rule 54, lawful option declined): a clause or rule passing on a recorded environmental covenant's activity or use limitations is lawful in Washington ('An environmental covenant may prohibit or restrict uses of real property that are authorized by zoning or by law other than this chapter.' (RCW 64.70.060)) but is not offered: the limits are site-specific and set by the agency, and the statute requires a copy only for 'Each person in possession of the real property subject to the covenant at the time the covenant is executed' (RCW 64.70.070(1)(c)), so no fixed lease text is required. The single-state covenant clauses (for example `environmental-covenant-va`, `environmental-covenant-notice-ut`) rest on their own statutes and are not tagged (rule 26). The education row says a landlord may add their own clause with advice.
- [G] ordnance-demolition-meter-disclosures (rule 54, lawful option declined): the pre-possession notice that keeps a new occupant from claiming local relocation assistance ('written notice from the property owner prior to taking possession of the dwelling unit that specifically describes the activity or condition that may result in their temporary or permanent displacement and advises them of their ineligibility for relocation assistance' (RCW 59.18.440(7)(b))) is lawful and useful only where a city or county has adopted a program under RCW 59.18.440(1) and a permit application is pending; the library offers no fixed text because the local program sets the details; the education row describes the notice. The single-state California clause `ordnance-demolition-meter-disclosures-ca` is not tagged (rule 26).
- [G] key-control-policy: the shared `lock-change-ak` (tagged for AK, SC, UT, MO, ID, MI, NM) is not tagged for Washington: it bars any lock change without the landlord's written agreement, which omits the right that 'the tenant may change or add locks to the tenant's dwelling unit at the tenant's expense' (RCW 59.18.575(4)) for a victim of sexual assault, stalking or unlawful harassment by the landlord. No Washington key-control clause is offered (no statute requires one; the tagged `keys` covers receipts, return and replacement). Item 8 answer for chapter 19.375 RCW recorded in `edu-key-control-policy-wa`: it reaches a landlord as a "person", but its notice-and-consent rule applies only to enrollment 'for a commercial purpose' (RCW 19.375.020(1)), and "Commercial purpose" excludes 'a security or law enforcement purpose' (RCW 19.375.010(4)). Consistency note for the slices answering `tenant-portal` and `tenant-confidential-information`: chapter 19.373 RCW (consumer health data, which lists 'Biometric data' (RCW 19.373.010(8)(b)(ix))) may also reach a landlord's biometric data; this row states the doubt and the cautious course.
- [G] recycling-notice (rule 54, lawful option declined): a lease or rules term telling tenants how to sort recycling and organics is lawful in Washington (it is a reasonable obligation under RCW 59.18.140(1) if brought to the tenant's attention at initial occupancy) but the library offers no fixed text: the only owner duty is a local option ('The governing body of each county or city may require the owners or operators of new or existing multifamily residential buildings to do any combination of the following' (RCW 70A.205.760)), so the content depends on the city or county. The single-state `recycling-notice-or` and `recycling-source-separation-ca` rest on their own statutes and are not tagged (rule 26).
- [G] waterbed (rule 54, lawful option already in the Washington lease): `common-area-use-wa` already requires the landlord's prior written consent for "a waterbed or other water-filled furniture", so the shared optional `waterbed-mn` (13 states) is not tagged for Washington: it would repeat that restriction in a second clause and add an aquarium-size prompt the Washington clause lacks. No waterbed deposit or fee clause is offered: any security is a deposit under RCW 59.18.260 and RCW 59.18.280, and a nonrefundable charge must be written as such (RCW 59.18.285). No new variable.
- [H] political-access: education only, no clause. No Washington statute requires or protects outsider political access to chapter 59.18 RCW housing, so there is nothing for a REQUIRED_DISCLOSURE; an outsider no-solicitation rule is the landlord's own choice under RCW 59.18.140 and the library's `common-area-use-wa` and `guest-policy` already carry the common-area and guest rules, so no separate solicitation clause is offered (rule 54: lawful, not offered, landlord may add their own rule with advice). The first pass answered this topic inside `edu-no-tenant-organizing-rule-wa` (WA battery 144); it now has its own row (rule 57), and the row adds RCW 29A.84.040 (sign removal) and the RCW 64.90.510(2) association sign rule, which the first pass did not cite for this topic.
- [H] religious-cultural-display: education only, no clause. `religious-display-nv` is a single-state Nevada clause resting on Nevada's statute (REQUIRED there) and is not tagged (rule 26); Washington has no display right to restate, so no REQUIRED_DISCLOSURE exists. A content-neutral display allowance is a lawful landlord choice; the library doesn't offer a WA clause for it because the shared Rules & Regulations rows already carry content-neutral rules and a door-item allowance would be a landlord policy choice (rule 54: lawful, not offered, landlord may add their own clause with advice).
- [H] ev-charging-requirements: the shared optional clause `ev-charger-installation-hi` (15 states) and `ev-charger-installation-conditions-nd` (ND, SC) were checked both ways and are not tagged for Washington (rule 54: a landlord-consent installation clause is lawful, since Washington gives no tenant right, but the library doesn't offer one here). Reasons: its bracketed option requiring the tenant to carry liability insurance naming Landlord as an additional insured shifts the cost of covering Landlord's own liability to the tenant, which risks the RCW 59.18.230(2)(f) bar on a tenant agreeing 'to indemnify the landlord for that liability or the costs connected therewith' (RCW 59.18.230(2)(f)) (no Washington case was searched); its metered-reimbursement option may make the meter a commercial measuring device needing registration under RCW 19.94.175(1)(h) and may be a resale of electricity that WAC 480-100-108(5) bars without tariff authority; and the `ev-charger-installation-conditions-nd` body is only a bracket line. The education row says the option exists and that a landlord may add their own agreement with advice. If Taylor wants a Washington installation clause later, it should drop the additional-insured bracket (or limit it to the tenant's own liability), bill electricity only as tariff and RCW 19.94 permit, and keep the deposit language tied to RCW 59.18.260 and RCW 59.18.270.
- [H] ev-charging-shared-area: `ev-charging-shared-area-co` (CO, IL, KS, MN, NE, TX, SC, MO) is not tagged for Washington after checking both ways. It reads as if the tenant may install in a shared area subject only to a reserve fee, though Washington gives no installation right; it makes "Tenant, and any tenant who later has exclusive rights to that space" responsible for any damage to the system or other property from its installation, maintenance, repair, removal or replacement, which reaches damage not caused by the persons RCW 59.18.060 names (`tenant-caused-damage-wa`) and purports to bind a later tenant who never signed this lease. Rule 54: a reserve-fee and damage clause for a consented installation is a lawful landlord option the library doesn't offer for Washington; the education row says so and points to the parking and common-area rows.
- [H] ev-charging-end-of-tenancy: `ev-charging-end-of-tenancy-co` (CO, IL, KS, MN, NE, NC; REQUIRED in its home states) is not tagged for Washington. Its first sentence ("A charging system installed at Tenant's expense remains Tenant's property") states as a rule what no Washington statute provides (only RCW 59.20.100 does so, for mobile home lots), and it conflicts with the WA-tagged `no-alterations` default that an approved alteration remains part of the property unless Landlord requires removal. Rule 54: an agreed ownership-and-removal term is a lawful landlord option; the library doesn't offer a Washington clause for it, and the education row tells the landlord to settle it in writing before installation, with advice.
- [H] tenant-portal: education only; no portal clause tagged. `tenant-portal-mn` (MN, SD, TX, GA, SC, NC, PA, MO, OK) contemplates Landlord requiring payment through the portal with an alternative only "whenever the portal is not working", which would make rent electronic-only in practice, barred by RCW 59.18.230(2)(j) ('Agrees to make rent payments through electronic means only' (RCW 59.18.230(2)(j))); not tagged. `tenant-portal-il` (IL, NY) nearly fits but adds a limit Washington law doesn't impose (no portal or electronic payment of "any other amount") and its bracket lets the landlord name a single non-electronic method, while Washington requires accepting personal check, cashier's check and money order and payment by mail (RCW 59.18.063(1)); not tagged. Washington's own `acceptable-payment-methods-wa` (which already lets the landlord list an online portal as an added method), `notices` and `electronic-signatures` carry the lease side, so a separate portal clause is a lawful option the library declines (rule 54); a landlord may add portal terms with advice.
- [H] tenant-confidential-information: education only. No statute requires lease text on the subject, and every shared row on this topic is a single-state education row, so nothing is tagged (rule 26). Item 8 (smart access and biometrics): chapter 19.375 RCW reaches a landlord as a 'person' (RCW 19.375.010(7)) but its notice-and-consent duty applies only to enrollment for a commercial purpose, and building-security enrollment is excluded (RCW 19.375.020(7)); the broader reach is chapter 19.373 RCW (consumer health data), which the row explains. `edu-smart-access-wa` stands unchanged; no other state's row is shared here. - [I] landlord-remedies-termination: rent acceleration is not offered as a Washington clause (rule 54). No statute authorizes or regulates it (WAG9000); where a tenant abandons, RCW 59.18.310(1)(b) sets a lesser-of measure, and 'any provision of a lease or other agreement, whether oral or written, whereby any section or subsection of this chapter is waived shall be deemed against public policy and shall be unenforceable' (RCW 59.18.230(1)(a)), so an acceleration clause risks being read as a waiver of that measure. Battery: WAG9000 (rent acceleration on a tenant default or on termination; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 8 hits (synthetic positive passed); T2 3 hits (real positive passed); T3 17 hits (real positive passed); T4 0 hits (synthetic positive passed); T5 2 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0). The education row says so and that a landlord may take advice on their own clause. Washington case law on acceleration and penalty clauses was not searched.
- [I] eviction-service-party: no Washington clause naming a person to accept eviction notices or process for the tenant is offered (rule 54; first log §18.2 "Not offered"). Reasons: RCW 59.12.040 directs service 'to the person entitled thereto' (RCW 59.12.040) and RCW 59.18.650(6)(a) requires that method; the summons follows RCW 4.28.080 through CR 4(d)(2); a lease substitute risks the RCW 59.18.230(1)(a) waiver bar; CR 4(g)(5) agent acceptance is a proof-of-service rule whose reach to a move-in lease appointment is a case-law question not searched. The row tells the landlord they may add a courtesy-copy clause with advice. - [I] forfeiture-redemption (WA log §7 item 7 settled): RCW 59.12.190 relief against forfeiture is treated as available to chapter 59.18 RCW tenants in addition to RCW 59.18.410(2) reinstatement and the RCW 59.18.410(3) stay. Grounds: RCW 59.18.420 excludes only RCW 59.12.090, RCW 59.12.100, RCW 59.12.121 and RCW 59.12.170; residential unlawful detainers proceed 'in accordance with chapter 59.12 RCW' (RCW 59.18.180(2)). Differences recorded in `edu-forfeiture-redemption-wa`: discretionary; any forfeiture (rent or covenant); verified petition within 30 days after judgment; condition is full payment of rent due or full performance; no payment plan. Left open as case-law questions (not searched): whether RCW 59.18.410's amount limits and three-notice bar carry over. Battery: WAG9005 (relief against forfeiture of a lease or tenancy after an unlawful detainer judgment; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 1 hit (real positive passed); T2 13 hits (real positive passed); T3 5 hits (synthetic positive passed); T4 1 hit (real positive passed); control 0).
- [I] cold-weather-vacate-notice: the shared `cold-weather-vacate-notice-wy` clause (winter heat floor, absence notice, entry during absence, freeze-damage charge; SERVES_LANDLORD in WY, SC, PA, IL, ID, WI) is not tagged for Washington. It states no Washington law (none exists, WAG9007); its entry sentence ("Landlord may enter the Premises during Tenant's absence as permitted by this Lease") would need Washington's limit that 'The landlord has no other right of access except by court order, arbitrator or by consent of the tenant' (RCW 59.18.150(7)), and its freeze-damage charge would need the RCW 59.18.060 fault persons; a Washington version is a drafting call for the main pass (rule 54 education row written: lawful to add with advice, not offered). Battery: WAG9007 (cold-weather or seasonal limits on eviction, writ execution or vacating, and a tenant cold-weather notice before vacating; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 25 hits (real positive passed); T2 2 hits (synthetic positive passed); T3 4 hits (synthetic positive passed); T4 3 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0).
- [I] trespass-notice: the shared `barred-person-covenant-ne` clause (SERVES_LANDLORD in NE, AZ, NC, AL, UT, MI, MT) is not tagged for Washington. Its victim carve-out reaches only a tenant who has sought a protective order, reported to police or obtained a qualified third-party certification, while Washington bars ending a tenancy 'based on the tenant's or applicant's or a household member's status as a victim of domestic violence, sexual assault, or stalking' (RCW 59.18.580(2)) and makes that a defense to the unlawful detainer (RCW 59.18.580(4)), so the clause could invite a termination Washington forbids for a victim who has done none of those things; a Washington version would also need to say that breach is enforced through the RCW 59.18.650(2)(b) 10-day notice. Recorded as a rule 54 option not offered (education row `edu-trespass-notice-wa` says the landlord may add one with advice). - [I] owner-move-in-reservation: the shared `owner-occupancy-termination-ar` clause (CONDITIONAL, SERVES_LANDLORD in 28 jurisdictions) is not tagged for Washington and no Washington reservation clause is offered (rule 54). The clause lets the landlord end a fixed Term early on notice of at least a bracketed number "not less than 30" days; Washington requires 90 days' advance written notice 'of the date the tenant's possession is to end' (RCW 59.18.650(2)(d)) and bars ending a fixed term early under (2)(d) unless the parties 'mutually consent, in writing, to ending the tenancy early and the tenant is afforded at least 60 days to vacate' (RCW 59.18.650(5)). Whether a move-in lease term is that consent is unsettled (no case searched) and the RCW 59.18.230(1)(a) waiver bar counsels against it.
