If the main pass prefers not to offer it in Washington, drop the tag; `edu-foreclosure-disclosure-wa` then needs its last sentences changed to say a landlord may add a disclosure with advice (rule 54).
- [I] Slice I note on the task's source list: RCW 59.18.375 (named for reading) is not in the current RCW. Chapter 59.18 RCW's own section table in the certified chapter file lists RCW 59.18.370 and then RCW 59.18.380, the corpus has no unit for it, and no RCW section or 2025-2026 session law cites it; no row relies on it. Its subject (rent paid into the court registry) is now covered for slice I by the payment-stay proviso of RCW 59.18.380 and by RCW 59.18.410(2).
- [I] Correction to the slice I note on RCW 59.18.375: its former subject was not read in this pass (the section is not in the corpus), so disregard the clause describing it as rent paid into the court registry; the note stands only for the absence of RCW 59.18.375 from the current chapter 59.18 RCW.
- [J] limitation-of-actions: no lease clause shortening or extending a limitation period is offered for Washington (rule 54). No statute authorizes one for real property leases (battery WAG10001 (limitation period specific to landlord or tenant claims, or a rule letting a lease shorten or extend a limitation period; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 52 hits (real positive passed); T2 4 hits (synthetic positive passed); T3 46 hits (real positive passed); T4 31 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0); the UCC permission in RCW 62A.2A-506(1) is limited to goods leases); RCW 59.18.280(3)(c) and RCW 59.18.670(4) set their own periods; enforceability of a contractual period is a case-law question not searched. The row tells the landlord the cautious course: treat three years from the end of the tenancy (one year with a fee in lieu of deposit) as the deadline for every money claim under rental agreements initiated on or after July 23, 2023.
- [J] actual-notice-method: no Washington clause adding an agreed notice method is offered (rule 54). Washington has no actual-notice rule to supplement (WAG10002 (actual notice in landlord-tenant law, or a rule letting the parties agree an added method of giving notice (Oregon ORS 90.150(4) type); whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules / chapters 59.18,59.12,59.04,59.08,59.16,59.20,1.80; T1 13 hits (real positive passed); T2 15 hits (synthetic positive passed); T3 8 hits (real positive passed); T4 18 hits (real positive passed); T5 14 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0)). Agreeing to electronic delivery is lawful only for notices whose governing law names no method and only while both parties have agreed to transact electronically; 'A party that agrees to conduct a transaction by electronic means may refuse to conduct other transactions by electronic means. The right granted by this subsection may not be waived by agreement.' (RCW 1.80.040(3)). The library declines a stand-alone electronic-notice consent clause because the eviction, termination and rent increase notices most landlords send have prescribed methods (RCW 59.12.040; `edu-notice-delivery-wa`) and the consent is revocable; a landlord may add its own consent clause with advice.
- [J] adverse-proceeding-notice: a tenant duty to forward court papers or official notices about the property is lawful in Washington (no statute bars or requires it, WAG10005 (landlord duty to tell tenants of a foreclosure, condemnation, tax sale, receivership or suit against the property, or tenant duty to forward notice of an adverse proceeding; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 50 hits (real positive passed); T2 19 hits (synthetic positive passed); T3 7 hits (synthetic positive passed); T4 17 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0)) but not offered (rule 54): RCW 61.24.040(1)(b)(vi), RCW 61.24.040(1)(e) and RCW 61.24.143 deliver foreclosure notices to occupants directly, and a damages or fee term attached to the duty must respect RCW 59.18.230(2)(e) and RCW 59.18.283(2). The single-state `tenant-notice-of-adverse-proceeding-nd` and `adverse-proceeding-notice-co` clauses rest on their own states' law and are not tagged (rule 26).
- [J] law-enforcement-cooperation: no Washington crime-free lease addendum or police-cooperation clause is offered (rule 54). Chapter 35.106 RCW regulates local crime-free rental housing programs (voluntary unless the RCW 35.106.020(3) threshold is met, no criminal-history bar, RCW 35.106.030) but requires no lease term; a tenant duty to cooperate with police has no statutory basis (WAG10007 (landlord or tenant duty to cooperate with or report to law enforcement, crime-free lease or addendum rules, and lease penalties for calling police; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 2 hits (synthetic positive passed); T2 5 hits (real positive passed); T3 33 hits (real positive passed); T4 0 hits (synthetic positive passed); T5 7 hits (real positive passed); T6 3 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0)); local program addenda are municipal law not held. A landlord may add its own clause with advice, keeping RCW 59.18.580 (victim status) and RCW 59.18.150(7) (no access without consent, order or emergency) in view.
- [J] eminent-domain: the shared optional clause `eminent-domain-vi` is tagged for Washington as written (rule 26). Screened both ways: it imposes no limit Washington lacks (no statute governs lease or rent on a taking, WAG10009 (effect of an eminent domain taking on a lease or rent (abatement, termination, apportionment) and lease allocation of a condemnation award; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 5 hits (real positive passed); T2 47 hits (synthetic positive passed); T3 50 hits (synthetic positive passed); T4 2 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0)) and gives away no chapter 59.18 RCW right; the award assignment is a contract term between the parties, with its effect on a lump-sum or apportioned award (RCW 8.04.110, RCW 8.12.150) resting on case law not searched; the relocation carve-out matches chapter 8.26 RCW.
- [J] construction-liens: the shared optional clause `no-liens-ar` is not tagged for Washington (rule 26 screened both ways). Its sentence that Tenant "will pay Landlord's reasonable costs caused by it" can be read to include attorneys' fees, and a Washington rental agreement may not provide that the tenant agrees to pay the landlord's attorneys' fees 'except as authorized in this chapter and awarded by a court pursuant to a judgment' (RCW 59.18.230(2)(e)); its unqualified duties for "any construction, alteration or repair" the tenant orders would also reach a contractor the tenant hires under the repair-and-deduct remedy (RCW 59.18.100(2)), a remedy a rental agreement may not waive (RCW 59.18.230(2)(a)). Washington has no lessor-protection or nonresponsibility statute (WAG10010 (construction liens for work a tenant orders: lessor interest, notice of nonresponsibility, no-lien lease clauses; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 66 hits (synthetic positive passed); T2 12 hits (synthetic positive passed); T3 35 hits (synthetic positive passed); T4 0 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0)), so a WA-specific clause would add no statutory effect. No WA clause is offered (rule 54); the education row tells landlords they may add their own clause with advice, limited to consented alterations and without fee shifting.
- [J] lease-term-limitation (prompt item 13): no lease term the library offers needs recording (RCW 65.08.060(1), (3) exclude leases of two years or less; recording a longer lease is optional and protects the tenant under RCW 65.08.070(1)). Spousal joinder: RCW 26.16.030(3) and RCW 6.13.060 speak of conveying or encumbering, not leasing, and the statutes do not settle whether a lease over one year conveys or encumbers (WAG10013 (maximum term for a residential lease, and spouse or domestic partner joinder (community property or homestead) for a lease; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 9 hits (real positive passed); T2 15 hits (synthetic positive passed); T3 7 hits (synthetic positive passed); T4 12 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0)); the cautious course recorded in `edu-lease-term-limitation-wa` is joinder by both spouses or domestic partners for community real property when the term with renewals or options exceeds one year, and for an owner-occupied homestead. Builder item: when a Washington lease's {{start_date}} to {{end_date}} term exceeds one year, prompt for the landlord's marital status and, if community property, a signature line for the spouse or domestic partner. No new variable is created; the prompt is a bracketed field.
- [J] usufructuary-lessor: no Washington clause stating the landlord's limited interest is offered (rule 54). Washington has no usufruct and no statute on leases by a life tenant (WAG10014 (usufruct, or leasing by a life tenant or other limited-interest holder and what happens to the lease when that interest ends; whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 0 hits (synthetic positive passed); T2 11 hits (real positive passed); T3 11 hits (real positive passed); T4 5 hits (real positive passed); control 0)); the Louisiana and Puerto Rico `usufructuary-lessor-la` and `usufructuary-lessor-pr` clauses rest on civil-law statutes and are not tagged (rule 26). The education row tells a limited-interest landlord to have the remainder holders join and to disclose the interest in writing; a landlord may add its own disclosure clause with advice.
- [J] Correction proposed for the main pass (slice J, not edited in place per the brief): in `edu-no-usufructuary-lessor-wa` bodyText, the sentence beginning "Co-owners: no Washington statute addresses a lease signed by only one of several co-owners" makes an absence claim that the topic's battery did not test (its terms search life estates and usufruct, not co-ownership). Proposed replacement: "Co-owners: if several people own the property, have every owner sign the lease; community property follows the spouse-joinder rules in `edu-lease-term-limitation-wa`."
- [J] Brief statements checked (slice J): (1) The task message's "RCW 60.04.011 owner definition" does not match the section: RCW 60.04.011 defines sixteen terms, including "Owner-occupied" (RCW 60.04.011(9)), but not "owner"; the lien attaches to 'the interest of the owner at whose instance' (RCW 60.04.051) the work was furnished. (2) The task message's gloss of RCW 26.16.030 ("both spouses join in a lease of community real property over one year") is not in the statute: 'Neither person shall sell, convey, or encumber the community real property without the other spouse or other domestic partner joining in the execution of the deed or other instrument by which the real estate is sold, conveyed, or encumbered' (RCW 26.16.030(3)); the section names no lease and no term, so `edu-lease-term-limitation-wa` states joinder for leases over one year only as the cautious course. (3) Registered agent: the duty to keep one is RCW 23.95.405 ('The following shall designate and maintain a registered agent in this state' (RCW 23.95.405)), not RCW 23.95.410, which is the address rule. (4) Recording is permissive, not required: a conveyance 'may be recorded' (RCW 65.08.070(1)), and leases of two years or less are not conveyances under RCW 65.08.060(3).
- [K] animal-care-access: the shared optional clause `animal-care-access-vi` is not tagged for Washington and no WA clause is offered (rule 54, declined). A tenant may consent to entry ('The landlord has no other right of access except by court order, arbitrator or by consent of the tenant.' (RCW 59.18.150(7))), but the clause works as a standing consent written into the lease, and Washington requires notice before non-emergency entry (RCW 59.18.150(6)) and makes a landlord liable for taking or detaining a tenant's property 'without the specific written consent of the tenant to such incident of taking or detention' (RCW 59.18.230(4)); whether a lease-signing consent meets either rule is a case-law question not searched. The education row `edu-animal-care-access-wa` tells the landlord the lawful routes (emergency entry, animal control, the death-of-tenant turnover in RCW 59.18.595(4)) and that a landlord may add its own clause with advice.
- [K] appliances-excluded: the shared optional clause `appliances-excluded-la` ("Items Not Included") is not tagged for Washington (rule 54, declined as doubtful). An item left in the unit for the tenant's use may be an appliance 'supplied by him or her' (RCW 59.18.060(8)); a clause that the landlord will not repair it would then waive RCW 59.18.060(8), and 'any provision of a lease or other agreement, whether oral or written, whereby any section or subsection of this chapter is waived shall be deemed against public policy and shall be unenforceable' (RCW 59.18.230(1)(a)); its saving sentence also implies a limitation of other landlord liability, which RCW 59.18.230(2)(f) bars. "Supplied" is undefined and no case was searched, so the cautious course is in the row: remove or maintain. A landlord may add its own clause with advice.
- [K] environmental-event-termination: the Colorado opt-in clause `environmental-event-termination-co` rests on Colorado law and is not tagged; no Washington clause is offered (rule 54, barred in effect). A landlord termination right after an environmental event adds a cause RCW 59.18.650(2) does not list: 'A landlord may not evict a tenant, refuse to continue a tenancy, or end a periodic tenancy except for the causes enumerated in subsection (2) of this section and as otherwise provided in this subsection.' (RCW 59.18.650(1)(a)); such a term waives that subsection and is unenforceable (RCW 59.18.230(1)(a)). Education row `edu-no-environmental-event-termination-wa` quotes the bar, matching the first pass's casualty-clause decision (WA log §6.1).
- [K] family-child-care: no Washington clause permitting or conditioning tenant family home child care is offered (rule 54, declined). No statute requires a landlord to allow it (battery WAG11016 (landlord duty to allow, or rule on, a tenant operating a licensed family home child care in a rental (Oregon-type); whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules; T1 0 hits (synthetic positive passed); T2 12 hits (real positive passed); T3 13 hits (synthetic positive passed); control 0)), so the shared `residential-use-only` clause (tagged for WA) already restricts commercial use; a landlord who allows child care should use its own written addendum with advice (license, insurance, indemnity, hours, common areas), because conditions vary by property and association rules (RCW 64.90.570) and local conditions (RCW 36.70A.450(2)) may also apply.
- [K] furnishings-included: optional Washington clause `furnishings-included-wa` offered (rule 54). The shared `furnishings-included-la` is not tagged because its return standard ("same condition, except for normal wear and tear") omits the exception in RCW 59.18.130(10) for 'conditions caused by failure of the landlord to comply with his or her obligations under this chapter' (RCW 59.18.130(10)). The WA clause points to the signed move-in checklist rather than repeating condition in the lease, because a deposit can't be kept for furnishings whose condition the checklist didn't document (RCW 59.18.280(1)(c)(iii)). Builder item: show this clause only when the property is rented furnished; no new variable (the item list is a [bracketed prompt]).
- [K] informal-dispute-resolution: the shared optional clause `informal-dispute-resolution-or` is tagged for Washington (rule 26) rather than a WA row written: RCW 59.18.315 permits written agreements to mediate, and the clause's carve-out for possession actions and its no-delay sentence keep it clear of RCW 59.18.230(1)(a) and RCW 59.18.230(2)(b). A binding arbitration clause is not offered; it is lawful only if 'the landlord pays the entire cost of the arbitration and the agreement is notarized' (RCW 59.18.230(2)(h)), and `edu-prohibited-lease-terms-wa` already states that bar. The Field Code question (a contract statute voiding stipulations restricting resort to the courts): no Washington counterpart was found, battery WAG11021 (contract or lease term restricting resort to the courts or requiring a pre-suit step, and statutes permitting or voiding it (Field Code and Oregon questions); whole RCW, held WAC titles, Constitution, 2025-2026 session laws, saved federal sections and saved state and local court rules / chapters 59.18,59.12,7.75,7.07,7.04A; T1 12 hits (synthetic positive passed); T2 2 hits (real positive passed); T3 1 hit (real positive passed); control 0); T1 hits are guardianship, interpreter and court-records rules on access to courts (RCW 11.130.310, RCW 11.130.320, RCW 11.130.430, RCW 2.43.090, RCW 7.105.245, Laws of 2025, ch. 55, Laws of 2026, ch. 177, GR 22, GR 31, GR 39, King and Snohomish local rules), T2 is RCW 59.18.230 and Laws of 2025, ch. 206, and T3 (limited to the landlord-tenant, dispute resolution center, mediation and arbitration chapters, since informal and pre-suit near dispute words statute-wide reaches every agency procedure) is RCW 7.75.010. The clause is drafted as a non-condition in any event.
- [K] Slice K housekeeping: one saved line was corrected in place before hand-back. The notes of `edu-no-emergency-contact-rule-wa` first described RCW 7.100.040 as a tenancy-free civil procedure statute; on re-reading, it is the mortgage servicer's nuisance-abatement notice to a property owner or authorized occupant, which gives a toll-free 24-hour number. The description now says that. The battery counts and the verdict are unchanged.
- [K] Brief lead checked (slice K): the task message listed RCW 64.90.475 as a possible tenant-notice rule. The section is headed "Accounts and records—Reconciliation" and governs association accounts, reconciliation and surplus funds for unit owners, so it holds no tenant or lease rule. The association topic rests instead on RCW 64.90.405(5), RCW 64.90.510(10)(c), RCW 64.90.565 and RCW 64.90.640 (`edu-no-association-disclosure-wa`).
- [L] periodic-services-entry (WA): a lease clause allowing entry for scheduled services on less than two days' written notice is barred (RCW 59.18.230(1)(a), RCW 59.18.230(2)(a); RCW 59.18.150(6)); `scheduled-services-entry-la` is not tagged for WA because it lets the landlord set notice as short as 24 hours by text or posting. A WA clause listing a service schedule with two days' written notice was declined: it would only restate `landlords-access-wa`, and whether one schedule notice covers later visits is unsettled, so the row tells the landlord to send a notice before each visit.
- [L] tenant-insurance-claims (WA): a mutual waiver of insured losses is barred as to the tenant's release of the landlord (RCW 59.18.230(2)(f)); a landlord-only waiver of its own claims or of its insurer's subrogation against the tenant is lawful but not offered (rule 54: a landlord's voluntary give-up that needs insurer consent and advice); the row says the landlord may add its own clause with advice.

### §5 Independent check

Rule 80, scaled as the prompt asks: at most two rounds, one checking agent at a time, each a fresh agent that had not written the rows, briefed only from the delivered delta and the primary sources (`CHECK-BRIEF.md`: check meaning, run one search of its own for every absence row, report fixes as exact old/new pairs, never edit the files). Fixes were applied by the main pass as errata, re-validated by the quote and citation checker on the assembled delta, and listed in the errata files in seq order.

**Round 1** covered every new WA row (176) and every tagged shared row (9), in two parts run one after the other:
- Part 1 (slices A-F, 86 items): 83 clean, 3 errors, 3 fixes (errata 15-17): `edu-habitability-materiality-wa` (the tenant's remedies follow duties under the act or the rental agreement, RCW 59.18.070, not statutory duties only), `edu-eviction-penalty-clause-ban-wa` (reinstatement until the eviction date with a pledge of financial assistance letter, RCW 59.18.410(2)), `edu-utility-allowance-cap-wa` (all three triggers of RCW 35.21.217(5)(a)).
- Part 2 (slices G-L and the five index rows, 99 items): 82 clean, 17 errors, 22 fixes (errata 18-39): `edu-asbestos-hazards-wa` (both limbs of the fine, RCW 49.26.016(1)); `edu-no-ev-charging-shared-area-wa` (publicly available equipment, RCW 19.94.550(1)(b), RCW 19.94.555(2)(b)); `edu-landlord-remedies-termination-wa` (pledge letter, duty to accept a pledge, 14-court-day suspension, RCW 59.18.410(2)); `edu-eviction-service-party-wa` (different times of day; regular and certified mail; nine days, RCW 59.18.055(1), (4)); `edu-limitation-of-actions-wa` (tolling only while the tenant departs from and resides out of the state, RCW 4.16.180); `edu-no-double-letting-rule-wa` (the delayed-possession clause covers only delay through no fault of the landlord); `edu-no-portfolio-thresholds-wa` (the third lien-notice exception, RCW 60.04.031(2)(c)); `edu-no-seasonal-tenancy-rule-wa` (the RCW 59.18.650(1)(b) route as well as (1)(c)); `edu-no-tenancy-at-will-wa` (absence claim corrected: RCW 64.12.020 uses the phrase; no statute creates or defines the tenancy); `edu-required-disclosures-wa` (holding-deposit receipt and written statement, RCW 59.18.253(2), (4)(a)); `edu-optional-lease-terms-wa` (both sentences of RCW 59.18.170(2); mediation is RCW 59.18.315, the tagged clause is an informal meeting step); `edu-confirmed-absences-habitability-wa` (pest duty is RCW 59.18.060(4)); and five rows that used a topic key as a row pointer (`edu-entity-landlord-court-representation-wa`, `edu-no-adverse-proceeding-notice-wa`, `edu-tenant-records-wa`, `edu-no-tpa-exemption-notice-wa`, `edu-no-tpa-notice-wa`). A main-pass sweep of every new row for the same pointer shape found one more (`edu-utility-apportionment-wa`, erratum 40).
- All nine tags were found lawful as Washington lease terms with accurate `WA:` segments; all three new clauses were found lawful with the right basis. The checkers' own absence searches (recorded per row in `check/r1-p1.jsonl` and `check/r1-p2.jsonl`) found one false absence claim (`edu-no-tenancy-at-will-wa`, above) and one in round 2 (below).
- Error shape: ten of the twenty rows with errors stated a rule with one of its triggers, limbs or extensions missing (Proposed SOP change 9); five used topic keys as row pointers (Proposed SOP change 8). Four narrower summaries that don't change what a landlord should do were reported and left as written (for example `edu-required-fees-wa` gives the 90-day increase notice without the 30-day rule for income-based subsidized tenancies, which only makes a landlord give more notice than needed).

**Round 2** re-checked the 21 rows edited after round 1 (`check/r2.jsonl`): 18 clean; every round-1 edit was found accurate and fitted to its sentence. Three rows had errors, two of them outside the edited text: `edu-limitation-of-actions-wa` (RCW 4.16.180 tolls the periods of chapter 4.16 RCW, and RCW 4.16.005 excepts periods set by other statutes, so the row now says not to rely on it for the three-year and one-year periods measured from the end of the tenancy); `edu-asbestos-hazards-wa` (the pipe exemption's surface-area condition and the record-keeping duty of an exempt project, RCW 49.26.125(1)(a)); `edu-no-portfolio-thresholds-wa` (absence claim partly false: WAC 162-38-070(4)(d) makes it an unfair practice for an owner of four or more units of rental property making nonstructural changes to fail to remove accessibility barriers where feasible; the row keeps its `edu-no-` id because the landlord-tenant act still has no portfolio tiers, and now states the exception as item (6)). Fixes are errata 41-48; the main pass re-read RCW 4.16.005, RCW 4.16.180, RCW 49.26.125 and WAC 162-38-070 against them.

### §6 Findings on existing rows

- `edu-no-tenant-rights-statement-wa`: Not wrong, incomplete: the row says "Some cities require a local rights handout" without the state condition on such rules. 'Any ordinance, resolution, or policy adopted by a city or county that imposes a requirement on landlords or sellers of real property, or their agents, to provide information to a buyer or tenant pertaining to the subject property or the surrounding area is effective only after' (RCW 64.06.080(1)) a summary and a link are posted on the municipal research website under RCW 43.110.030(2)(e). Proposed added sentence (optional, a pointer rather than a new duty): "A city or county rule requiring you to give tenants information about the property or the surrounding area takes effect only after it is listed on the state-contracted municipal research website (`edu-no-rights-responsibilities-guide-wa`)."
- `edu-smart-access-wa`: Not an error: `edu-smart-access-wa` states the RCW 59.18.750 through 59.18.760 duties correctly but does not mention the two general biometric privacy chapters. Chapter 19.375 RCW reaches a landlord only for commercial-purpose enrollment ('Nothing in this section requires an entity to provide notice and obtain consent to collect, capture, or enroll a biometric identifier and store it in a biometric system, or otherwise, in furtherance of a security purpose.' (RCW 19.375.020(7))), and chapter 19.373 RCW may reach it ('Biometric data' (RCW 19.373.010(8)(b)(ix)) is consumer health data; collection only 'With consent from the consumer for such collection for a specified purpose' (RCW 19.373.030(1)(a)(i)) or as necessary for a requested service). Proposed wording to append to the row's body: "Biometric entry may also bring the state's consumer health data and biometric identifier laws; see `edu-key-control-policy-wa`." The duty itself is stated in the new row, so no proposed_row.
- `edu-towing-wa`: The row says "On residential property you may have an unauthorized vehicle impounded immediately" and "the 24-hour sign rule applies only to nonresidential property" without saying what residential property means. The towing chapter defines it narrowly: '"Residential property" means property that has no more than four living units located on it.' (RCW 46.55.010(8)). An apartment complex with five or more units is therefore nonresidential private property, where 'No person may impound, tow, or otherwise disturb any unauthorized vehicle standing on nonresidential private property or in a public parking facility for less than twenty-four hours unless a sign is posted near each entrance and on the property' (RCW 46.55.070(1)). A landlord of a larger complex reading the row could tow immediately without signs. Proposed wording for the first sentence: "On residential property (property with no more than four living units) you may have an unauthorized vehicle impounded immediately by giving a registered tow truck operator a signed written authorization containing the statutory liability statement. On a property with five or more units, a vehicle may be towed in less than 24 hours only if signs are posted near each entrance and on the property showing the times vehicles may be impounded and the towing firm's name, telephone number and address."
- `edu-conversion-notice-wa`: The row states the 120-day notice and the limits on requiring tenants to vacate but omits the tenant's purchase offer, a declarant duty on the same topic: 'For 60 days after delivery or mailing of the notice described in subsection (1) of this section, the person required to give the notice must offer to convey each unit or proposed unit occupied for residential use to the residential tenant or residential subtenant who leases that unit.' (RCW 64.90.655(2)(a)), with the 180-day better-terms rule and 10-day acceptance window in RCW 64.90.655(2)(a)(i)-(ii) and the same offer in RCW 64.34.440(2) (effective until January 1, 2028). The row says only "To convert apartments to condominiums, the declarant must give each residential tenant at least 120 days' notice". It also omits the option a city or county may adopt requiring relocation assistance of up to three months' rent for lower-income tenants who don't buy (RCW 64.90.655(6)(e)). Proposed added sentences: "For 60 days after the notice you must offer to sell each occupied unit to its tenant. If the tenant doesn't buy, for the next 180 days you may offer that unit to anyone else on better price or terms only after offering the same price and terms to the tenant in writing and giving the tenant 10 days to accept. Your city or county may also require you to pay relocation assistance of up to three months' rent to lower-income tenants who don't buy."

### §7 Findings for other states

- All states (federal): the Fair Credit Reporting Act disposal rule applies to every landlord who uses tenant screening reports: 'Any person who maintains or otherwise possesses consumer information for a business purpose must properly dispose of such information by taking reasonable measures to protect against unauthorized access to or use of the information in connection with its disposal.' (FEDERAL: 16 C.F.R. § 682.3(a)), with consumer information defined to include any record 'that is a consumer report or is derived from a consumer report' (FEDERAL: 16 C.F.R. § 682.1(b)) (authority FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 1681w(a)(1)). The library has a federal row for the adverse-action notice (`edu-fcra-adverse-action-notice`) but none for disposal; a federal education row like it, tagged for every state, would close the gap. Washington's row carries the rule since errata 9-10.

### Proposed SOP changes

1. **Rule 19 (battery log with parallel helpers).** When two canvass agents run at once, give each its own battery log file and its own id range (here WAG main 1-999, slice A 1000s, B 2000s and so on), and have the battery tool refuse an id outside the slice's range or already used in any log. A single shared log invites two agents to take the same next id. Proposed text: "With more than one canvass agent, each writes its own battery log and draws ids from a range assigned in the brief; the tool refuses reused or out-of-range ids."
2. **Rule 24 (currency screen by section, not by chapter).** The session-law screen here first filtered the index by chapter, and slice E found an act (Laws of 2026, ch. 137) that amends sections in chapters the filter did not list (RCW 57.08.081, RCW 54.16.285, RCW 87.03.015). Proposed text: "Screen the session-law index for every section a row relies on (each section's own number, not only its chapter), and read each hit."
3. **Rule 24 (live cross-check normalization).** Statutory forms print blanks as dot leaders, and the live page and the corpus can print different numbers of dots. Proposed text: "Compare the live page and the corpus by a hash of the whitespace-free body; where a section contains a form, also strip runs of dots or underscores, and say so."
4. **Rule 60 (citation checker coverage).** The quote checker's citation pattern first missed titles with a letter suffix (Title 62A, RCW 62A.2-302), and one helper wrote that limitation into a row's notes (removed by erratum 1). Proposed text: "The checker's citation pattern must accept lettered titles, chapters and sections (62A, 35A, 70A) and hyphenated UCC-style section numbers; a tool limitation is never written into a row."
5. **Rule 36 (a "not held" claim in a brief).** The main pass's brief told helpers the Protecting Tenants at Foreclosure Act was not on the saved FEDERAL: 12 U.S.C. § 5220 page; it was there as a statutory note, and one helper repeated the error in a row (fixed by erratum 8). Proposed text: "Before a brief or row says a source is not held, grep the saved files for the act's popular name and public law number, not only its code section; statutory notes often sit on a section's page."
6. **Rule 12 (federal sections fetched mid-canvass).** Federal sections a helper asks for can be saved during the canvass, but adding them to the index while helpers are still running changes the hit counts of batteries already run. This pass held them aside until every helper had handed back, then added them, applied the errata, and reported the count changes on replay. Proposed text: "Save federal FETCH requests when they arrive, but add them to the search index only after the last canvass helper hands back; then apply the resulting errata and report the changed battery counts in §3."
7. **Rule 27 (helper edits to their own saved lines).** Three helpers found and corrected errors in their own saved lines before hand-back, outside the brief's "edit only to fix check errors" rule, and two others left corrections for the main pass to apply as errata. Proposed text: "A helper may correct its own saved line before hand-back if it records the change in a decision object (old text, new text, why); the main pass re-checks it like any other line."
8. **Rule 59 (pointers to topics vs rows).** The independent check found five rows that put a topic key in backticks as if it were a row id (for example `tpa-sunset` for `edu-tpa-sunset-wa`); the validator accepted them because it accepts topic keys too. Proposed text: "A backticked pointer is a row id; a topic key in backticks must carry the word 'topic' beside it, and the validator flags any other backticked token that is not a row id."
9. **Rule 79 (every limb of a rule).** Ten of the twenty errors the independent check found stated a rule with one of its triggers or limbs missing (the reinstatement window without the pledge-letter extension, a utility deduction with one of its three triggers, a remedy for statutory duties without duties under the rental agreement, a fine with one of its two limbs). Proposed text: "Before a row summarizes a rule, list the section's triggers, exceptions and extensions as a checklist in the helper's scratch notes and tick each one off in the body or say why it doesn't matter to a landlord; the checker brief names this error shape."


### Proposed topic questions

All three go under existing keys, so no new gaps open in other states.
1. `key-control-policy`: "Does a state biometric or consumer health data privacy law reach a landlord's fingerprint, face or other biometric door-entry system?" (Washington: chapter 19.375 RCW applies only to enrollment for a commercial purpose and excludes security; chapter 19.373 RCW's reach to a landlord is unsettled.)
2. `limitation-of-actions`: "Does the landlord-tenant act set its own deadline for a landlord's suit against a former tenant, shorter than the general contract period?" (Washington: three years from the end of the tenancy for sums above the deposit, RCW 59.18.280(3)(c); one year with a fee in lieu of deposit, RCW 59.18.670(4).)
3. `required-disclosures`: "Does a state law make local landlord-disclosure ordinances effective only after a state posting?" (Washington: RCW 64.06.080(1) and RCW 43.110.030(2)(e).)

## Federal row: disposing of screening reports (Claude Code, 2026-11-20)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-20 (Washington gaps log §7, backlog item 9): one federal education row, `edu-fcra-disposal-rule`, is tagged here and in every jurisdiction, read from the official text (16 C.F.R. part 682 on eCFR; 15 U.S.C. § 1681w on govinfo.gov). It says that anyone who keeps a screening, credit or background report, or a record taken from one, for a business purpose must dispose of it with reasonable measures against unauthorized access (16 C.F.R. § 682.3), with the rule's examples, and that the rule sets no retention period (§ 682.4). This state's existing rows stay; no clause text changed.

## Shared edit: lead-based paint agent's statement (Claude Code, 2026-11-20)

From the West Virginia gaps sync (WV gaps log §6 item 21 and §7; backlog items from the Nevada and Puerto Rico syncs): the shared `lead-based-paint` clause gained a bracketed agent's statement and a sentence requiring each person who makes a statement to sign and date it, the two elements the federal rule requires in every lease of pre-1978 housing (40 C.F.R. § 745.113(b)(5)-(6); HUD's twin rule, 24 C.F.R. § 35.92(b)(5)-(6); both read on eCFR 2026-11-20). Uniform federal edit under rule 62: the same in every jurisdiction, no state term changed; it applies only where an agent acts for the landlord.

## Federal rows: the federal pass (Claude Code, 2026-11-20)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-20 (backlog item 13; scope: private rentals and voucher tenancies, not federally assisted or tax-credit properties): nine federal education rows are tagged here and in every jurisdiction, each read from the official text (eCFR, govinfo.gov, the Federal Register; 109 quotations matched): `edu-scra-eviction-protections` (50 U.S.C. §§ 3951, 3931, 3958; the 2026 rent limit of $10,542.60), `edu-bankruptcy-eviction-federal` (11 U.S.C. § 362(a), (b)(22)-(23), (k), (l), (m)), `edu-fair-housing-coverage-federal` (42 U.S.C. §§ 3602(k), 3603(b), 3604(c), 3607(b)(1), 1982), `edu-fair-housing-harassment-federal` (24 C.F.R. §§ 100.600, 100.7; 42 U.S.C. § 3617), `edu-accessible-design-federal` (42 U.S.C. § 3604(f)(3)(C), (f)(4), (f)(7)), `edu-debt-collectors-federal` (15 U.S.C. § 1692a(5)-(6)), `edu-asbestos-demolition-renovation-federal` (40 C.F.R. §§ 61.141, 61.145), `edu-antenna-rights-federal` (47 C.F.R. § 1.4000) and `edu-voucher-repairs-and-rent` (24 C.F.R. §§ 982.404, 982.451, 982.453, 982.507, 982.510). A tenth, `edu-credit-reporting-furnisher-duties` (15 U.S.C. §§ 1681s-2, 1681i(a)(1); 12 C.F.R. §§ 1022.40-1022.43), was added the same day at Taylor's request. ## Model-law comparison: two new topics (Claude Code, 2026-11-21)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-20: compare the library's topic list with the uniform model laws (the 1972 Uniform Residential Landlord and Tenant Act, the Revised URLTA of 2015, and UCC § 3-311) and answer any topic they raise that the library lacked. Two did: checks marked 'paid in full' (`paid-in-full-payments`) and a general duty of good faith (`good-faith-obligation`). This jurisdiction's rows are `edu-paid-in-full-checks-wa` (VERIFIED; RCW 62A.3-311; RCW 62A.1-308(b)) and `edu-good-faith-obligation-wa` (VERIFIED; RCW 59.18.020). Each was researched from official text by an independent agent; every quotation was machine-checked against the saved page and the quotes are in each row's notes.
